HOW TO USE THIS FILE
  1. Run the phases in order. Phase 1 builds the shared back-navigation mechanism, the MediaFrame image piece and the zoom-safe shell that later phases rely on.
  2. For each phase, copy everything between
       -----8<----- BEGIN PHASE N PROMPT -----
     and
       -----8<----- END PHASE N PROMPT -----
     into a NEW Claude Code or Antigravity conversation. Each prompt is self-contained: scope (B), audit (C), implementation (D), responsiveness (E), images (F), back navigation (G), functional and API work (H), bug fixing (I), tests (J), acceptance (K) and the standing rules are all inside the markers.
  3. Execute one phase per conversation. The agent stops at the end of the phase and reports.
  4. Phases marked "OWNER APPROVAL" add design-silent UI. Their prompt tells the agent to confirm with you first.

DECISION NEEDED BEFORE PHASE 1 (see the QA report, section 4)
  The project has only Vitest in a node environment: no DOM and no browser runner. Real browser-zoom tests therefore can't be automated without a new dev dependency.
  Option A (default in these prompts): the zoom and layout matrix is run by the agent with a browser tool and recorded in the QA report; Vitest covers the logic.
  Option B: approve adding @playwright/test (one devDependency). Phases then also add Playwright specs that emulate each zoom (viewport = window / zoom, deviceScaleFactor = zoom).
  Every prompt says "only if Playwright has been approved and installed", so both options work without editing the prompts.

GLOBAL BACK-NAVIGATION MATRIX (the target behaviour every phase implements)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Format: path taken -> Back -> expected. The fallback (direct entry or refresh with no in-app history) is in [ ].
  Chats -> Conversation -> Back -> Chats                                   [INBOX]
  Matches -> Conversation -> Back -> Matches                               [INBOX]
  Match detail -> Conversation -> Back -> Match detail
  Conversation -> Plan date (Venues) -> Back -> Conversation               [the member's conversation]
  Venues -> Place -> Back -> Venues (same area, category and scroll)       [VENUES for the member]
  Place -> Time -> Back -> Place; Time -> Review -> Back -> Time
  Review -> Send -> Plan: Back from Plan -> Conversation (the sent review is replaced, not reopened)
  Conversation -> Play game -> Back -> Conversation (GAME-LEAVE during a question)  [the member's conversation]
  Discover -> Profile -> Back -> Discover (same deck card)                 [DISCOVER]
  Likes Received -> Profile -> Back -> Likes Received (same scroll)        [LIKES]
  Compatibility -> View profile -> Back -> Compatibility
  Discover -> Filters -> Save -> Discover (replace; Back does not reopen the saved filters)  [DISCOVER]
  Stories -> Story -> Back -> Stories (scroll restored)                    [STORIES]
  Story -> Report -> Back -> Story;  Date complete -> Share story -> Back -> Date complete
  Notifications -> target (plan, story, chat, match) -> Back -> Notifications  [the target's parent]
  Header bell -> Notifications -> Back -> the page you were on             [DISCOVER]
  Header heart -> Likes Received -> Back -> the page you were on           [DISCOVER]
  Header avatar -> Profile -> Back -> the page you were on                 [DISCOVER]
  Profile -> Edit -> Back -> Profile; Profile -> Photos -> Back -> Profile; Edit -> Photos -> Back -> Edit
  Profile -> Trust -> Activity -> Back -> Trust -> Back -> Profile
  Settings -> any sub-page -> Back -> Settings                             [SETTINGS]
  Profile -> Change location -> Back -> Profile                            [SETTINGS]
  Settings -> Membership -> Plans -> Back -> Membership -> Back -> Settings
  Quota (from chat) -> Plans -> Back -> Quota -> Back -> Conversation
  Checkout: Payment done -> replace -> Membership (Back does not reopen checkout)
  Block / Delete / Sign out: replace (Back never reopens the finished step)
  Onboarding: Back follows data/onboarding/flow.ts (unchanged, rule 35)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TOP KNOWN ISSUES FOUND IN PHASE 0 (details and file references in the QA report)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  N1  Many Back targets are fixed in data:
        - INBOX -> DISCOVER (data/portal/chats.ts:34);
        - the shared shell Back -> DISCOVER (data/portal/shell.ts:19), used by matches-view, filters-view, compat-view, chat-photo and chat-voice;
        - games -> INBOX (data/portal/games.ts:83);
        - notifications -> DISCOVER (data/portal/account.ts:442);
        - membership -> PROFILE (data/portal/membership.ts:143).
      Only discover-view uses router.back().
  N2  Date "Change area" opens onboarding LOCATION (data/portal/dates.ts:124 and :129).
  N3  After the date has passed, the Attendance view ("Have you met?") replaces the date details. DateSummary is rendered only in PlanDetails (src/components/sections/portal/dates/plan-view.tsx:102); Attendance starts around line 155.
  N4  Conversation tools are a 4-pill grid (AI help / Photo / Voice / Safety, chat-view.tsx:569-573). Plan date / Play game sit in a separate CHAT-ATTACH chooser. There is no "+" menu and no three-dot conversation menu.
  N5  Chat bubbles are full width (chat-bubble.tsx: "flex w-full"), the own / incoming sides aren't aligned left and right, the timestamp line is always shown, and photo attachments are forced to aspect 4:5 with object-cover.
  N6  The story photo is a fixed h-87.5 (350 px) / max-md h-60 box with object-cover (story-body.tsx), so uploads are cropped. Feed thumbnails are object-cover too.
  N7  The "Flow support" column is hidden from 768 to 1279 px (workspace.tsx), which is 110-175 % zoom at 1440. On STORIES it holds "Have a story to tell?" / My shared story, so the primary action can become unreachable.
  N8  The portal main column is capped at max-w-480 (1920 px) while the header is full width. At 50 % zoom (2880 CSS px) this leaves grey bands of about 480 px on each side.
  N9  Fixed-px grids at xl: chat 280 / fill / 312; discover 240 / 448 / fill; own profile 439 / fill; hero minmax(560px, ...). Fixed-width ActionButton widths reach 300 px. These need checking at 1280-1440 (100-110 %) and at 360 px (400 %).
  N10 The fixed phone tab bar and the fixed panes (760 px, floor 420 px) are risky in 225-300 px tall viewports (300-400 % zoom).
  N11 There is no Likes Received page, route or service, although GET /discovery/likes-received and its match / pass endpoints exist in the collection.
  N12 There is no unmatch endpoint in the Postman collection (backend blocker for the Unmatch menu item).
  N13 POST /matches/:id/venue-suggestions takes no location, only a category, so the server can't search around a date-specific area (backend blocker; Phase 15 has the frontend workaround).
  N14 The story API returns no image width / height, so the ratio is measured on load (backend recommendation: imageWidth / imageHeight).
  N15 Vitest has no DOM or browser runner, so zoom tests are manual unless Playwright is approved.

PHASE 0 - Project Audit and Baseline
Purpose:  Understand the architecture, inventory every route and API, establish the test baseline, add the permanent standards and write this roadmap and the QA report.
Priority: P0 - prerequisite (DONE 2026-11-16)

B. SCOPE
  - Whole repository, read-only except for: AGENTS.md (new rules 46-58), CLAUDE.md (pointer section), NEW_FRONTEND_IMPROVEMENT_ROADMAP.txt and NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md.
  - Routes: every folder under src/app (see the QA report's route inventory).
  - APIs: postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (User folder), postman/48Date-WebSockets.postman_collection.json, src/lib/api/endpoints.ts.
C. INITIAL AUDIT
  - Read AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, README.md, package.json, vitest.config.ts, src/lib/routes.ts, src/components/sections/portal/shared/portal-page.tsx, src/lib/api/endpoints.ts and src/app/globals.css.
  - Enumerate the screen codes, client states, back targets in src/data/portal/*.ts, image usages (grep "<Image"), fixed grid columns and widths, and the endpoints the frontend doesn't call.
  - Preserve everything. No code changes in Phase 0.
D. IMPLEMENTATION
  - Run npm test, npm run lint and npm run build, and record the numbers.
  - Append AGENTS.md rules 46-58 under the heading "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" (after rule 45, keeping all existing content).
  - Add the pointer section to CLAUDE.md.
  - Write NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md and this roadmap.
E. RESPONSIVENESS
Zoom levels (all mandatory): 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 % at a 1440x900 window, then spot-check 50 %, 200 % and 400 % at a 1920x1080 window. Also check the rule 3 widths 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920. (Equivalent CSS viewports are in standing rule 3.)
At every zoom, record and fix:
  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;
  - focus ring and keyboard order broken at high zoom.
  Phase 0 only RECORDS the known zoom risks (N7-N10). It fixes nothing.
F. IMAGES
  Phase 0 only records the image inventory and the risks (N5, N6, N14). No changes.
G. BACK NAVIGATION
  Phase 0 only records the fixed back targets (N1) and writes the global matrix above.
H. FUNCTIONAL AND API
  Phase 0 maps the endpoints to services and lists the endpoints the frontend doesn't call: discovery likes-received / boost / undo / feed, matching/me, dates/:id/history, ai quota, notification delete, message edit / delete, comment delete and push token.
I. BUG FIXING
  Record only; fixing happens in the phases.
J. TEST CASES
Existing tests to run UNCHANGED (baseline 2026-11-16: 3 files, 54 tests, all passing): src/lib/api/api-layer.test.ts (12), src/lib/portal/portal-logic.test.ts (10), src/test/api-contract.test.ts (32), plus every *.test.ts added by earlier phases. Never edit, skip, weaken or delete them. Then run npm run lint and npm run build (baseline: both clean).
  No new tests in Phase 0.
K. ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
  [ ] The baseline is recorded: 54/54 tests, lint clean, build clean.

STANDING RULES (apply to every step of this phase)

0. Context. Repo: c:\Users\fardin\Downloads\TECHREION\48date-user, the 48Date user web frontend. Stack: Next.js 16.4 App Router (cacheComponents / partial prerendering, React Compiler, Turbopack), React 19.3, Tailwind CSS v4, TanStack Query v5, socket.io-client, Vitest 3 (node environment). This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. When a fix needs the backend, record it under "Backend/API blockers" in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md: the endpoint, the current behaviour, the needed behaviour and the frontend workaround (if any).

1. Read first:
   - AGENTS.md: binding rules 1-45, the "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" rules 46-58, the Pieces registry and the Decisions list;
   - CLAUDE.md and README.md;
   - the bundled Next.js guide in node_modules/next/dist/docs/ for every Next API you touch (this Next version has breaking changes);
   - NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md, to see what earlier phases did. Do NOT edit the older FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md.

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.
   - When a test fails: find the cause, fix the frontend, and re-run the unchanged test. If the cause is the backend, record it as a blocker.
   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.
   - Zoom and layout checks that Vitest can't run go into the QA report as a manual matrix with their results (or into Playwright specs, only if Playwright has been approved and installed).
   - Finish with npm test, npm run lint and npm run build. All must pass with no new warnings.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;
    - bugs found and bugs fixed;
    - tests executed, passed and failed (the real numbers from the run);
    - the zoom matrix results and the back-navigation results;
    - backend/API blockers and remaining issues.
    Also update the phase table. Never claim a test passed without running it.

WORK ORDER
  1. Read the rules (1).
  2. Audit the scope. Write the findings into the report before changing code.
  3. Implement D-H.
  4. Write the new tests (J).
  5. Run the zoom matrix, the back-navigation checks and the functional and multi-account checks.
  6. Run npm test, npm run lint and npm run build, and fix frontend failures until they pass.
  7. Update the report.
  8. STOP. Summarise the changed files, the test numbers and the blockers, then wait for the next phase instruction.

  - commit or push.
Inspect only this phase's scope first.

PHASE 1 - Global Portal Shell, Shared Layout, Back-Navigation and Image Infrastructure
Purpose:  Fix the zoom behaviour of everything every portal page shares, and build the history-aware Back and the ratio-preserving MediaFrame that later phases use.
Priority: P0 - blocking (all later phases depend on it)

C. INITIAL AUDIT
  - Measure the shell at all 13 zoom levels. Note the grey bands (N8), header / main misalignment, tab-bar overlap at 225-300 px tall viewports (N10), dialogs taller than the viewport, ActionButton fixed widths (control-sizes.ts: setupPrimary 240, profileAction 300, summaryAction 240, notifyAction 224, findMore 216, passAction 200, portalAction 180 ...) overflowing at 360 px, and the aside hidden from 768 to 1279 (N7).
  - Inventory every PortalBack / ActionLink "portalBack" caller (about 100; grep "PortalBack") and every router.replace / router.push in src/components/sections/portal.
  - Preserve: the Figma geometry at 1440 (80 px gutters, 1280 column), W11 tablet decisions, the phone tab bar below 640, SessionGate / SetupGate redirects, rule 16 Reveal behaviour and reduced motion.
D. IMPLEMENTATION
  1. Back-navigation mechanism (rules 51-53):
     a. nav-trail.ts keeps a per-tab list of visited in-app paths (pathname, without query) in sessionStorage under the key "48date.nav.trail", validated on read and capped at 50 entries. "48date.*" keys are already cleared by authService.signOut, so sign-out resets the trail.
        API:
          record(path)        push, or pop when path equals the previous entry (browser Back)
          markReplace()       the next record replaces the last entry
          previous(current)   the previous entry, or null
          clear()
        Register clear with onCacheClear if that fits the pattern in lib/api/cache.ts.
     b. navigation-tracker.tsx: a "use client" piece using usePathname(), calling record() on every path change. Mount it once in src/app/layout.tsx inside Suspense if the Next 16 docs require it with cacheComponents.
     c. use-back-target.ts: useBackTarget(fallback: Destination) returns { href, onBack }. If previous(current) exists and differs from the current path, onBack calls router.back() (the browser restores scroll and Next restores client state). Otherwise it calls router.push(fallback href). href is always the fallback, so middle-click, no-JS and "copy link" still work.
     d. A replaceRoute(router, href) helper that calls markReplace() and then router.replace(href). Every router.replace in portal sections must use it (grep and switch them all).
     e. PortalBack gains history-first behaviour by default: it renders the ActionLink with the fallback href and intercepts the click through useBackTarget. Give the Action type an optional "back: 'fixed'" flag for the few places that must always go to their data target (for example "Back to welcome" after deletion). Keep the onClick prop (earlier client state) working exactly as now.
     f. Do NOT change data targets yet, except to make sure every portal Back has a sensible fallback. Each feature phase verifies its own routes against the matrix.
     g. Unit tests: push, pop on browser Back, replace, the cap, invalid storage, previous() at the first entry, and the fallback when there is no history.
  2. Shell zoom fixes:
     a. One column token shared by the header inner row, main and the tab bar, so their edges align at every width (rule 47). At >=1920 CSS px (zoom <=75 % at 1440), let the column grow (for example max-w-[min(100%,2560px)] registered as a token) and let page grids add columns instead of leaving bands. Reading-width text keeps its own max line length.
     b. HeaderMenu: replace the fixed w-24 with an intrinsic width (gap-2) so a third control (the heart icon from Phase 10) fits. Check the header at 360 px: logo + 3 x 44 px controls must fit without overflow.
     c. PortalTabBar: in short viewports (@media (max-height: 420px)) use a compact variant (icons only, with labels kept as aria-label / sr-only), and make main's bottom padding equal to the bar's real height through a CSS variable, so nothing is covered.
     d. PortalDialog / ui/dialog: content max-h-[calc(100dvh-2rem)] (as a token), with the body overflow-y-auto and the header and actions always visible. Check it at 360x225.
     e. ActionButton fixed widths: make each width "w-full max-w-[token]" or "min-w-0 flex-1 basis-[token]" so buttons shrink inside 360 px while keeping the Figma width when there is room. ActionRow stacks below sm (it already does); verify.
     f. Workspace: the "Flow support" aside stays hidden 768-1279 (W11), but add a disclosure (a "Details" button or a section below the panel) wherever an aside holds an action that appears nowhere else. Keep the generic mechanism here; feature phases decide per page. Check that min-w-0 is on the grid children.
     g. globals.css: confirm body overflow-wrap; add a ".break-anywhere" utility (overflow-wrap:anywhere) for user text; confirm no global min-width.
  3. MediaFrame (rule 50):
     a. aspect.ts: ratioOf(width, height) returns a number; clampRatio(r, min 9/21, max 21/9) is for the frame only (the image is never cropped, because it is contained); cssAspect(r) returns a string. Unit-test 16:9, 9:16, 4:3, 3:4, 1:1, 21:9, 1:3, zero and NaN.
     b. media-frame.tsx ("use client"): props src, alt, width?, height?, maxHeight (a token), sizes, rounded, revealDelay, className. When width / height are known, the box gets aspect-ratio from them at once. Otherwise it reserves a neutral 4:3 placeholder and switches to the measured ratio from onLoad (naturalWidth / naturalHeight). The image is next/image fill with object-contain, so it is never cropped or stretched. Neutral background (a tones.ts token); unoptimized via isRawImage. Add it to the Pieces registry.
E. RESPONSIVENESS
Zoom levels (all mandatory): 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 % at a 1440x900 window, then spot-check 50 %, 200 % and 400 % at a 1920x1080 window. Also check the rule 3 widths 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920. (Equivalent CSS viewports are in standing rule 3.)
At every zoom, record and fix:
  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;
  - focus ring and keyboard order broken at high zoom.
  Phase-specific:
  - The header row, main column and tab bar edges align at every zoom.
  - No grey band wider than the designed gutter at 50-400 %.
  - The tab bar never covers content at 300-400 %.
  - Dialog actions stay visible at 360x225.
  - Buttons fit at 360 px.

A bug caused by the backend is written into "Backend/API blockers" with the endpoint, the observed response, the expected response and the frontend workaround.

STANDING RULES (apply to every step of this phase)

0. Context. Repo: c:\Users\fardin\Downloads\TECHREION\48date-user, the 48Date user web frontend. Stack: Next.js 16.4 App Router (cacheComponents / partial prerendering, React Compiler, Turbopack), React 19.3, Tailwind CSS v4, TanStack Query v5, socket.io-client, Vitest 3 (node environment). This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. When a fix needs the backend, record it under "Backend/API blockers" in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md: the endpoint, the current behaviour, the needed behaviour and the frontend workaround (if any).

1. Read first:
   - AGENTS.md: binding rules 1-45, the "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" rules 46-58, the Pieces registry and the Decisions list;
   - CLAUDE.md and README.md;
   - the bundled Next.js guide in node_modules/next/dist/docs/ for every Next API you touch (this Next version has breaking changes);
   - NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md, to see what earlier phases did. Do NOT edit the older FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md.

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.
   - When a test fails: find the cause, fix the frontend, and re-run the unchanged test. If the cause is the backend, record it as a blocker.
   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;

PHASE 2 - Landing Page and Public Sections
Purpose:  Make the public landing page and its section URLs correct at every zoom, with the composition kept at 1920.
Priority: P2

B. SCOPE

C. INITIAL AUDIT
  - Display type is fluid on 100vw. At 400 % zoom (360 CSS px) display-240 resolves to its 140 px minimum and display-96 to 46 px; check that hero headlines don't overflow or clip at 360-480 px.

  - Hero grid (lg:grid-cols-[minmax(560px,683fr)...] in hero.tsx or site-nav.tsx, whichever holds it): at 1024-1152 px (125-140 % zoom), 560 px plus the second column must fit.

D. IMPLEMENTATION
  1. Fix every overflow or clipping found at the 13 zoom levels: use min() inside grid minimums (for example minmax(min(560px,100%),683fr)), wrap long display words, and let illustration cards scale (max-w-full, aspect-ratio).
  2. Make sure no landing element has a fixed width wider than 360 px without max-w-full.
  3. At 50-75 %, keep the 1536 column (design) and verify that full-bleed backgrounds and imagery fill the width. If a section shows a flat empty band (a background that isn't full-bleed), make the background full-bleed.
  4. Check the sticky / floating nav in short viewports (225-300 px tall): it must not cover more than about 25 % of the height. At max-height 420 px, make it static or compact.
  5. ScrollToSection: the section lands below the floating nav (scroll-margin-top as a token) at every zoom.
E. RESPONSIVENESS
Zoom levels (all mandatory): 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 % at a 1440x900 window, then spot-check 50 %, 200 % and 400 % at a 1920x1080 window. Also check the rule 3 widths 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920. (Equivalent CSS viewports are in standing rule 3.)
At every zoom, record and fix:
  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;
  - focus ring and keyboard order broken at high zoom.
  Phase-specific: hero headline, quote card, store badges and FAQ at 360-480 px; the gallery row; footer columns stack without overflow; the nav Menu is usable with the keyboard at 400 %.
F. IMAGES
  - Hero background (preload, cover); hero collage photos (eager, PhotoCard with aspect from data); safety photo with pinned overlays; download phone screenshots (keep their intrinsic ratio, object-contain, never stretched); step-card images; illustration avatars (1:1).
  - Test at 360, 768, 1440 and 2880 CSS px: no distortion, faces not cropped off, phones not squashed.
G. BACK NAVIGATION
  - No in-page Back button (Figma). Check the browser history instead: / -> /how-it-works (section link, scroll={false}) -> browser Back returns to / at the previous scroll position; / -> /signup -> browser Back returns to the landing page at the same scroll.
  - Direct entry to /how-it-works scrolls to that section; a refresh keeps it.
H. FUNCTIONAL AND API
  - Nav links, footer links, FAQ accordion (multiple open, keyboard, reduced motion), CTAs to WELCOME / SIGNIN, App Store / Google Play as notFunctional (aria-disabled with reason).
  - The API checklist is N/A (static page). Confirm no API requests in the network panel.
I. BUG FIXING
Find and fix EVERY frontend bug inside this phase's scope, not only those listed: layout, state, logic, accessibility, API mapping, loading / empty / error states, stale cache after writes, race conditions, console errors and warnings, wrong copy wiring and broken links. Before changing a shared piece or helper, grep every caller and re-test them. A bug outside the scope is written into the QA report "Remaining issues", not fixed here. A bug caused by the backend is written into "Backend/API blockers" with the endpoint, the observed response, the expected response and the frontend workaround.
J. TEST CASES
Existing tests to run UNCHANGED (baseline 2026-11-16: 3 files, 54 tests, all passing): src/lib/api/api-layer.test.ts (12), src/lib/portal/portal-logic.test.ts (10), src/test/api-contract.test.ts (32), plus every *.test.ts added by earlier phases. Never edit, skip, weaken or delete them. Then run npm run lint and npm run build (baseline: both clean).
  New tests:
  - src/lib/routes.landing.test.ts: every anchor in data/landing/anchors.ts builds a valid hrefFor("L02", id); sectionFromParam accepts each anchor and rejects unknown or nested values.
  - A data test that every landing image has alt text and width / height (or an aspect) defined.
  Zoom: the full matrix on /, plus /how-it-works direct entry at 100 % and 400 %.
  Navigation: the browser-history checks in G.
K. ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
Generic acceptance (all must hold):
  [ ] Every route and state in scope passes the 13-level zoom matrix and the rule 3 widths: no horizontal overflow, no unintended bands, no clipping, the primary action reachable. The results table is in the QA report.
  [ ] Every Back in scope behaves as listed in G, including direct entry and refresh.
  [ ] Every image in scope keeps its intended ratio, with no stretching and no unintended cropping (or the phase says why images are N/A).
  [ ] Every API-driven view in scope shows real API data and has loading, empty and error states. Writes update the UI from the server response or by invalidating the cache.
  [ ] The new tests listed in J exist and pass. All existing tests pass unchanged.
  [ ] npm test, npm run lint and npm run build pass with no new warnings.
  [ ] The AGENTS.md Pieces registry and Decisions are updated for every new piece or design-silent choice.
  [ ] NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md is updated (phase section and phase table). Backend/API blockers are listed separately.

STANDING RULES (apply to every step of this phase)

0. Context. Repo: c:\Users\fardin\Downloads\TECHREION\48date-user, the 48Date user web frontend. Stack: Next.js 16.4 App Router (cacheComponents / partial prerendering, React Compiler, Turbopack), React 19.3, Tailwind CSS v4, TanStack Query v5, socket.io-client, Vitest 3 (node environment). This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. When a fix needs the backend, record it under "Backend/API blockers" in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md: the endpoint, the current behaviour, the needed behaviour and the frontend workaround (if any).

1. Read first:
   - AGENTS.md: binding rules 1-45, the "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" rules 46-58, the Pieces registry and the Decisions list;
   - CLAUDE.md and README.md;
   - the bundled Next.js guide in node_modules/next/dist/docs/ for every Next API you touch (this Next version has breaking changes);
   - NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md, to see what earlier phases did. Do NOT edit the older FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md.

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.
   - When a test fails: find the cause, fix the frontend, and re-run the unchanged test. If the cause is the backend, record it as a blocker.
   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.
   - Zoom and layout checks that Vitest can't run go into the QA report as a manual matrix with their results (or into Playwright specs, only if Playwright has been approved and installed).
   - Finish with npm test, npm run lint and npm run build. All must pass with no new warnings.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;
    - bugs found and bugs fixed;
    - tests executed, passed and failed (the real numbers from the run);
    - the zoom matrix results and the back-navigation results;
    - backend/API blockers and remaining issues.
    Also update the phase table. Never claim a test passed without running it.

  1. Read the rules (1).
  2. Audit the scope. Write the findings into the report before changing code.

  4. Write the new tests (J).
  5. Run the zoom matrix, the back-navigation checks and the functional and multi-account checks.

       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.
   - When a test fails: find the cause, fix the frontend, and re-run the unchanged test. If the cause is the backend, record it as a blocker.
   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.
   - Zoom and layout checks that Vitest can't run go into the QA report as a manual matrix with their results (or into Playwright specs, only if Playwright has been approved and installed).
   - Finish with npm test, npm run lint and npm run build. All must pass with no new warnings.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;
    - bugs found and bugs fixed;
    - tests executed, passed and failed (the real numbers from the run);
    - the zoom matrix results and the back-navigation results;
    - backend/API blockers and remaining issues.
    Also update the phase table. Never claim a test passed without running it.

PHASE 4 - Authentication (Sign up, Sign in, OTP, Google, Session ended)
Purpose:  Auth screens that work at every zoom, with correct step Back and real API behaviour.
Priority: P1

B. SCOPE
  - Routes: /signup (WELCOME), /signup/verify (OTP), /signup/verified (PHONE-VERIFIED), /signup/google, /signup/google/error, /signin (SIGNIN), /signin/verify (OTP-SIGNIN), /signin/verified, /signin/google, /signin/google/error, /signin/session-ended (SESSION).
  - Client states (rule 32): PHONE-ERROR, OTP-FILLED, OTP-ERROR, OTP-RESEND, OTP-EXPIRED, OTP-LIMIT, VERIFY-PROCESS.
  - Components: src/components/sections/auth/* (auth-page, auth-route, phone-step, verify-step, verified-step, google-step, message-step, sign-out, dev-otp-hint); pieces auth-shell (photo panel >=1024 with the lg grid column clamp(400px, calc(59.62vw - 210.5px), 648px); logo header below), auth-photo-panel, phone-field (country selector), otp-field (6 cells), form-header, or-divider, consent-note, session-gate (guest).
  - Service: lib/auth/auth-service.ts (api-auth-service / mocks).
  - APIs: POST /auth/phone/request-otp, POST /auth/phone/verify, POST /auth/google, POST /auth/refresh, POST /auth/logout, GET /onboarding (setup status after verify).
C. INITIAL AUDIT
  - At 110-175 % zoom on 1440 (1309 down to 823 CSS px) the photo panel switches at 1024; check that the form column never drops below its 400-ish px content.
  - OTP cells at 360 px (8 px gaps); the country selector list at 225 px height; the dev OTP hint and its Copy button wrapping; the session-ended message.
  - Check the focus order and the error announcements.
  - Preserve: the phone in sessionStorage only, the verify URL without a number returning to the entry, Google rendered only with a client ID, the devOtp hint (TESTING ONLY), and SessionGate guest forwarding members to DISCOVER.
D. IMPLEMENTATION
  1. Fix every overflow or clipping at the 13 zoom levels in AuthShell and the steps. The photo panel hides below 1024 (existing); make sure the form panel is centred with 16 px gutters and no bands at 50 %. At 2880 px, decide whether the form column grows or the photo panel scales (keep the Figma ratio), and record it in Decisions.
  2. The OTP field shares the width at 360 px with no horizontal scroll. The cells keep a 44 px minimum tap target, or the field reflows.
  3. The country picker opens as a scrollable list inside the viewport at short heights.
  4. The primary button (Continue / Verify) stays reachable at 400 % (the shell scrolls; no fixed-height containers).
E. RESPONSIVENESS
Zoom levels (all mandatory): 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 % at a 1440x900 window, then spot-check 50 %, 200 % and 400 % at a 1920x1080 window. Also check the rule 3 widths 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920. (Equivalent CSS viewports are in standing rule 3.)
At every zoom, record and fix:
  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;
  - focus ring and keyboard order broken at high zoom.
  Phase-specific: photo-panel switch points at 110 / 125 / 150 %; OTP at 360 px; the Google button width at 360 px.

  - The Bangladesh flag image in phone-field (size-5).

  - Direct entry to /signin/verify without a number goes to /signin (existing).
  - Members visiting /signin are forwarded to DISCOVER.
  - These auth Backs keep their flow targets (they are steps). Make sure they don't use the portal trail incorrectly.
H. FUNCTIONAL AND API

  - Google: only if a client ID is configured; otherwise verify the "Google sign-in isn't set up" path.
I. BUG FIXING
Find and fix EVERY frontend bug inside this phase's scope, not only those listed: layout, state, logic, accessibility, API mapping, loading / empty / error states, stale cache after writes, race conditions, console errors and warnings, wrong copy wiring and broken links. Before changing a shared piece or helper, grep every caller and re-test them. A bug outside the scope is written into the QA report "Remaining issues", not fixed here. A bug caused by the backend is written into "Backend/API blockers" with the endpoint, the observed response, the expected response and the frontend workaround.

0. Context. Repo: c:\Users\fardin\Downloads\TECHREION\48date-user, the 48Date user web frontend. Stack: Next.js 16.4 App Router (cacheComponents / partial prerendering, React Compiler, Turbopack), React 19.3, Tailwind CSS v4, TanStack Query v5, socket.io-client, Vitest 3 (node environment). This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. When a fix needs the backend, record it under "Backend/API blockers" in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md: the endpoint, the current behaviour, the needed behaviour and the frontend workaround (if any).

1. Read first:
   - AGENTS.md: binding rules 1-45, the "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" rules 46-58, the Pieces registry and the Decisions list;

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".
