STANDING RULES (apply to every step of this phase)

0. Context. Repo: c:\Users\fardin\Downloads\TECHREION\48date-user, the 48Date user web frontend. Stack: Next.js 16.4 App Router (cacheComponents / partial prerendering, React Compiler, Turbopack), React 19.3, Tailwind CSS v4, TanStack Query v5, socket.io-client, Vitest 3 (node environment). This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. When a fix needs the backend, record it under "Backend/API blockers" in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md: the endpoint, the current behaviour, the needed behaviour and the frontend workaround (if any).

1. Read first:
   - AGENTS.md: binding rules 1-45, the "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" rules 46-58, the Pieces registry and the Decisions list;
   - CLAUDE.md and README.md;
   - the bundled Next.js guide in node_modules/next/dist/docs/ for every Next API you touch (this Next version has breaking changes);
   - NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md, to see what earlier phases did. Do NOT edit the older FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md.

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.
   - When a test fails: find the cause, fix the frontend, and re-run the unchanged test. If the cause is the backend, record it as a blocker.
   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.
   - Zoom and layout checks that Vitest can't run go into the QA report as a manual matrix with their results (or into Playwright specs, only if Playwright has been approved and installed).
   - Finish with npm test, npm run lint and npm run build. All must pass with no new warnings.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;
    - bugs found and bugs fixed;
    - tests executed, passed and failed (the real numbers from the run);
    - the zoom matrix results and the back-navigation results;
    - backend/API blockers and remaining issues.
    Also update the phase table. Never claim a test passed without running it.

  - Contract tests for POST /files, the photos step, POST /verification and the verification snapshot (new file).
  Zoom: the matrix on PHOTO-SOURCE, PHOTO-PREVIEW (portrait and landscape), VERIFY-CAPTURE (mock camera allowed) and NOTIFY.
  Navigation: G.
K. ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
Generic acceptance (all must hold):
  [ ] Every route and state in scope passes the 13-level zoom matrix and the rule 3 widths: no horizontal overflow, no unintended bands, no clipping, the primary action reachable. The results table is in the QA report.
  [ ] Every Back in scope behaves as listed in G, including direct entry and refresh.
  [ ] Every image in scope keeps its intended ratio, with no stretching and no unintended cropping (or the phase says why images are N/A).
  [ ] Every API-driven view in scope shows real API data and has loading, empty and error states. Writes update the UI from the server response or by invalidating the cache.

STANDING RULES (apply to every step of this phase)

0. Context. Repo: c:\Users\fardin\Downloads\TECHREION\48date-user, the 48Date user web frontend. Stack: Next.js 16.4 App Router (cacheComponents / partial prerendering, React Compiler, Turbopack), React 19.3, Tailwind CSS v4, TanStack Query v5, socket.io-client, Vitest 3 (node environment). This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. When a fix needs the backend, record it under "Backend/API blockers" in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md: the endpoint, the current behaviour, the needed behaviour and the frontend workaround (if any).

1. Read first:
   - AGENTS.md: binding rules 1-45, the "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" rules 46-58, the Pieces registry and the Decisions list;
   - CLAUDE.md and README.md;
   - the bundled Next.js guide in node_modules/next/dist/docs/ for every Next API you touch (this Next version has breaking changes);
   - NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md, to see what earlier phases did. Do NOT edit the older FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md.

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.
   - When a test fails: find the cause, fix the frontend, and re-run the unchanged test. If the cause is the backend, record it as a blocker.
   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.
   - Zoom and layout checks that Vitest can't run go into the QA report as a manual matrix with their results (or into Playwright specs, only if Playwright has been approved and installed).
   - Finish with npm test, npm run lint and npm run build. All must pass with no new warnings.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;
    - bugs found and bugs fixed;
    - tests executed, passed and failed (the real numbers from the run);
    - the zoom matrix results and the back-navigation results;
    - backend/API blockers and remaining issues.
    Also update the phase table. Never claim a test passed without running it.

WORK ORDER
  1. Read the rules (1).
  2. Audit the scope. Write the findings into the report before changing code.
  3. Implement D-H.
  4. Write the new tests (J).
  5. Run the zoom matrix, the back-navigation checks and the functional and multi-account checks.

  7. Update the report.
  8. STOP. Summarise the changed files, the test numbers and the blockers, then wait for the next phase instruction.

PHASE 7 - Discover Deck and Match Moment
Purpose:  A discovery deck whose Like / Pass / Super Like stay reachable at every zoom, with real API swipes and matches.
Priority: P1

B. SCOPE
  - Routes: /discover. Client states: SWIPE-PASS, SWIPE-LIKE, SWIPE-SUPER, DISCOVER-NEXT, MATCH-NEW, DISCOVER-EMPTY, FILTERS-EMPTY.
  - Components: src/components/sections/portal/discover/discover-view.tsx (xl grid 240px / 448px / minmax(0,1fr); lg minmax(0,400px) / 1fr), discover-sidebar.tsx (preferences column), profile-overview.tsx; pieces swipe-card (draggable, 96 px threshold), status-screen, status-artwork, action-button (decision / superLike widths).
  - Libraries: lib/portal/deck-intent.ts, portal-service.ts (+ api-portal-service, mock).
  - APIs: GET / PUT /discovery/preferences (sidebar facts), POST /discovery/runs, GET /discovery/runs/current, GET /discovery/runs/:id, GET /discovery/runs/:id/events (SSE), GET /discovery/runs/:id/cards (10 per page, next page below 3), POST /discovery/swipes (LIKE / PASS / SUPER_LIKE; 429 weekly Super Like limit), GET /matches/unseen, POST /matches/:id/seen.
C. INITIAL AUDIT
  - At 100 % on 1440 the xl grid has fixed 240 + 448 columns. At 110 % (1309 px) check that the third column has room.
  - From 1024 to 1279 (110-140 %) the preferences span the top (W11: no preferences column; "Adjust filters" in the heading).
  - At 250-400 %: does the card plus the decision buttons fit in 225-360 px of height? Like / Pass must be reachable without scrolling past a huge photo.
  - The drag gesture at high zoom; feedback announcements; MATCH-NEW layout at 360x225.
  - Preserve: the decision feedback timing (0.8 s), the drag thresholds, the announcements, the 429 message under the buttons keeping the card, deck pagination rules, background matches on MATCH-NEW.
D. IMPLEMENTATION
  1. Replace fixed xl columns with clamp-based columns that hold the Figma sizes at 1440 but shrink between 1280 and 1440, for example grid-cols-[clamp(200px,16.7vw,240px)_clamp(360px,31vw,448px)_minmax(0,1fr)] registered as tokens; or switch the breakpoint at which the overview column drops.
  2. Swipe card photo height: aspect 4:5, with max-h set to the viewport (calc(100dvh - header - decision row - gaps) as a token) so the decision row is always visible. At max-height 420 px, keep the decision row sticky under the card.
  3. Decision buttons: shrink (min-w-0 flex-1) at 360 px with icons and accessible labels kept.
  4. MATCH-NEW and the empty states: the StatusScreen actions are visible at 360x225 (dialog or page scroll).
E. RESPONSIVENESS
Zoom levels (all mandatory): 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 % at a 1440x900 window, then spot-check 50 %, 200 % and 400 % at a 1920x1080 window. Also check the rule 3 widths 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920. (Equivalent CSS viewports are in standing rule 3.)
At every zoom, record and fix:
  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;
  - focus ring and keyboard order broken at high zoom.
  Phase-specific: at 400 % the card, Pass, Super Like and Like are visible together in the viewport, or Like / Pass are sticky; the sidebar and overview stack below the deck; no horizontal scroll while dragging.
F. IMAGES
  - Swipe card main photo: a 4:5 portrait, object-cover, object-position centre-top (faces); the drag transform must not stretch it.
  - The match-moment photos (StatusArtwork 96 px photo, 1:1).
  - Test API photos of portrait, landscape and square source ratios: no distortion, faces visible.
  - The member placeholder image for a missing photo.
G. BACK NAVIGATION
  - DISCOVER is a hub (no Back in its main state).
  - MATCH-NEW Back = keep discovering (the existing client state).
  - DISCOVER-EMPTY / FILTERS-EMPTY Back = history (existing router.back(); migrate it to useBackTarget with the DISCOVER fallback so a direct entry doesn't leave the app).
  - Discover -> Profile -> Back returns to the same card. Verify the deck position is kept (deck state in the TanStack cache / session state).
  - Discover -> Filters -> Save -> Discover is a replace.
H. FUNCTIONAL AND API
  - Like / Pass / Super Like by button and by drag; keyboard access to the decisions; next page of cards; an unavailable candidate skipped; run progress over SSE; 429 shown and card kept; mutual like -> MATCH-NEW (also from /matches/unseen) and the seen mark.
  - Multi-account:
      M2 (FREE) likes F6 and F6 likes M2 back: MATCH-NEW appears for F6 immediately and for M2 on the next unseen check.
      M2 uses Super Like until the weekly limit (429) and sees the message.
    Record the pairs used (they become test matches for later phases).
I. BUG FIXING
Find and fix EVERY frontend bug inside this phase's scope, not only those listed: layout, state, logic, accessibility, API mapping, loading / empty / error states, stale cache after writes, race conditions, console errors and warnings, wrong copy wiring and broken links. Before changing a shared piece or helper, grep every caller and re-test them. A bug outside the scope is written into the QA report "Remaining issues", not fixed here. A bug caused by the backend is written into "Backend/API blockers" with the endpoint, the observed response, the expected response and the frontend workaround.

Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.

   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.
   - Zoom and layout checks that Vitest can't run go into the QA report as a manual matrix with their results (or into Playwright specs, only if Playwright has been approved and installed).
   - Finish with npm test, npm run lint and npm run build. All must pass with no new warnings.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;

  4. Write the new tests (J).

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.

  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;
  - focus ring and keyboard order broken at high zoom.
  Phase-specific: the photo column and facts stack below lg; the photo grid uses auto-fill; the decisions stay reachable at 400 %.
F. IMAGES
  - Main photo: 4:5 cover, centre-top. Additional photos: their frame ratio, cover, centre-top. The photo-tint overlay must not distort.
  - Test portrait, landscape and square API photos plus the placeholder.
G. BACK NAVIGATION
  - Discover -> Profile -> Back = Discover (same card).
  - Likes Received -> Profile -> Back = Likes Received.
  - Compat -> View profile -> Back = Compat.
  - Conversation context card -> Profile -> Back = Conversation.
  - Match detail -> Profile -> Back = Match detail.
  - Fallback: DISCOVER.
  - Decisions return to DISCOVER (existing) with replace, so Back doesn't reopen the decided profile.
H. FUNCTIONAL AND API
  - Profile data from GET /discovery/profile/:nickname (no sample copy with the API); interests in common; the unavailable member; Like / Pass; COMPAT-PENDING until interests are saved.
  - Multi-account: M3 views F7's profile and compatibility (shared interests shown match both profiles).
I. BUG FIXING
Find and fix EVERY frontend bug inside this phase's scope, not only those listed: layout, state, logic, accessibility, API mapping, loading / empty / error states, stale cache after writes, race conditions, console errors and warnings, wrong copy wiring and broken links. Before changing a shared piece or helper, grep every caller and re-test them. A bug outside the scope is written into the QA report "Remaining issues", not fixed here. A bug caused by the backend is written into "Backend/API blockers" with the endpoint, the observed response, the expected response and the frontend workaround.
J. TEST CASES
Existing tests to run UNCHANGED (baseline 2026-11-16: 3 files, 54 tests, all passing): src/lib/api/api-layer.test.ts (12), src/lib/portal/portal-logic.test.ts (10), src/test/api-contract.test.ts (32), plus every *.test.ts added by earlier phases. Never edit, skip, weaken or delete them. Then run npm run lint and npm run build (baseline: both clean).
  New tests:
  - src/lib/portal/shared-interests.test.ts: intersection by id and label; empty.
  - A contract test for GET /discovery/profile/:nickname, including unavailable (new file).
  Zoom: the matrix on DISCOVER-PROFILE and COMPAT.
  Navigation: all the G entries.
K. ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
Generic acceptance (all must hold):
  [ ] Every route and state in scope passes the 13-level zoom matrix and the rule 3 widths: no horizontal overflow, no unintended bands, no clipping, the primary action reachable. The results table is in the QA report.
  [ ] Every Back in scope behaves as listed in G, including direct entry and refresh.
  [ ] Every image in scope keeps its intended ratio, with no stretching and no unintended cropping (or the phase says why images are N/A).
  [ ] Every API-driven view in scope shows real API data and has loading, empty and error states. Writes update the UI from the server response or by invalidating the cache.

STANDING RULES (apply to every step of this phase)

0. Context. Repo: c:\Users\fardin\Downloads\TECHREION\48date-user, the 48Date user web frontend. Stack: Next.js 16.4 App Router (cacheComponents / partial prerendering, React Compiler, Turbopack), React 19.3, Tailwind CSS v4, TanStack Query v5, socket.io-client, Vitest 3 (node environment). This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. When a fix needs the backend, record it under "Backend/API blockers" in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md: the endpoint, the current behaviour, the needed behaviour and the frontend workaround (if any).

1. Read first:
   - AGENTS.md: binding rules 1-45, the "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" rules 46-58, the Pieces registry and the Decisions list;
   - CLAUDE.md and README.md;
   - the bundled Next.js guide in node_modules/next/dist/docs/ for every Next API you touch (this Next version has breaking changes);
   - NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md, to see what earlier phases did. Do NOT edit the older FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md.

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.
   - When a test fails: find the cause, fix the frontend, and re-run the unchanged test. If the cause is the backend, record it as a blocker.
   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.
   - Zoom and layout checks that Vitest can't run go into the QA report as a manual matrix with their results (or into Playwright specs, only if Playwright has been approved and installed).
   - Finish with npm test, npm run lint and npm run build. All must pass with no new warnings.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;
    - bugs found and bugs fixed;
    - tests executed, passed and failed (the real numbers from the run);
    - the zoom matrix results and the back-navigation results;
    - backend/API blockers and remaining issues.
    Also update the phase table. Never claim a test passed without running it.

  8. STOP. Summarise the changed files, the test numbers and the blockers, then wait for the next phase instruction.

PHASE 11 - Matches, Outcomes and Match Details
Purpose:  Match lists, outcome tabs and match details with server-true countdowns, correct Back and responsive cards.
Priority: P1

B. SCOPE
  - Routes: /matches (MATCHES, MATCHES-EMPTY, "Looking for matches..." during auto-matching), /matches/cancelled (MATCHES-CANCELLED), /matches/complete (MATCHES-COMPLETE), /matches/match/<nickname> (MATCH-EXPIRING, MATCH-EXPIRED, active match, confirmed date).
  - Components: src/components/sections/portal/matches/matches-view.tsx and match-detail-view.tsx; pieces match-card (240 px photo, name, badge, last message, 3 actions), outcome-card (240 px image), status-tabs (one URL each), status-badge (live={false}), status-artwork, countdown; hooks use-match-clock, use-now; lib/portal/server-clock.ts, api-match-lookup.ts.
  - APIs: GET /matches?tab=&cursor&limit, GET /matches/:id, GET /matches/unseen, POST /matches/:id/seen, POST /matching/runs, GET /matching/runs/:id/events (SSE). Unused but relevant: GET /matching/me (auto-matching status, tips, nextRetryAt); Phase 36 owns adding it unless the owner wants it here.
C. INITIAL AUDIT
  - Match cards 3 -> 2 -> 1 columns: at 50 % (2880 px), does the grid add columns or leave bands? The 240 px fixed photo height at narrow card widths; the three actions at 360 px; the tabs wrapping at 400 %.
  - Countdown text not in a live region; match detail with expiresAt null ("Active match" without a countdown).
  - Preserve: server-owned deadlines (meta.serverTime correction), the outcome copy decisions, MATCHES-EMPTY "Find matches for me".
D. IMPLEMENTATION
  1. Grid: repeat(auto-fill, minmax(min(100%, a card-min token), 1fr)) so 50 % zoom shows more columns and 400 % shows one.
  2. Match and outcome card photos: aspect-ratio instead of a fixed 240 px height (keep 240 at the Figma width through the aspect).
  3. Card actions: wrap or stack inside the card at narrow widths, with no overflow.
  4. Status tabs: horizontal scroll-snap or wrap at 400 %, with aria-current kept.
  5. Match detail: the workspace and the hidden aside (W11) keep key info (countdown, plan) visible on tablets.
E. RESPONSIVENESS
Zoom levels (all mandatory): 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 % at a 1440x900 window, then spot-check 50 %, 200 % and 400 % at a 1920x1080 window. Also check the rule 3 widths 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920. (Equivalent CSS viewports are in standing rule 3.)
At every zoom, record and fix:
  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;
  - focus ring and keyboard order broken at high zoom.
  Phase-specific: the grid column counts at each zoom; tabs at 360 px; the match detail StatusArtwork (36 px timer) at 360x225.
F. IMAGES
  - Match card: portrait 4:5 (or the Figma 240-tall frame expressed as a ratio), cover, centre-top. Outcome card: the Figma frame ratio, cover. Detail artwork photo: 96 px, 1:1.
  - Test portrait, landscape and square API photos and the placeholder.
G. BACK NAVIGATION
  - The /matches Back currently uses the shell back fixed to DISCOVER; make it history-first with the DISCOVER fallback.
  - Matches -> Match detail -> Back = Matches, the same tab (/matches, /matches/cancelled or /matches/complete).
  - Matches -> Conversation -> Back = Matches.
  - Match detail -> Conversation / Plan / Profile -> Back = Match detail.
  - Fallbacks: detail -> MATCHES (data/portal/matches.ts:88).
  - The tab is a URL, so it survives refresh.
H. FUNCTIONAL AND API
  - Lists per tab with cursor pagination; the unseen marking; countdown equal on both accounts (server time); expired -> MATCH-EXPIRED; message / plan / details actions; auto-matching progress over SSE.
  - Multi-account: the M2 <-> F6 match from Phase 7 / 10 shows the same remaining time for both (within 1 s), and a message preview updates after a message is sent.
I. BUG FIXING
Find and fix EVERY frontend bug inside this phase's scope, not only those listed: layout, state, logic, accessibility, API mapping, loading / empty / error states, stale cache after writes, race conditions, console errors and warnings, wrong copy wiring and broken links. Before changing a shared piece or helper, grep every caller and re-test them. A bug outside the scope is written into the QA report "Remaining issues", not fixed here. A bug caused by the backend is written into "Backend/API blockers" with the endpoint, the observed response, the expected response and the frontend workaround.
J. TEST CASES
Existing tests to run UNCHANGED (baseline 2026-11-16: 3 files, 54 tests, all passing): src/lib/api/api-layer.test.ts (12), src/lib/portal/portal-logic.test.ts (10), src/test/api-contract.test.ts (32), plus every *.test.ts added by earlier phases. Never edit, skip, weaken or delete them. Then run npm run lint and npm run build (baseline: both clean).
  New tests:
  - src/lib/portal/server-clock.test.ts: offset from meta.serverTime; the countdown never negative; expiresAt null -> active without a timer.
  - A contract test for GET /matches tab mapping and GET /matches/:id with latestDate.place (new file).
  Zoom: the matrix on /matches (0 / 1 / many), /matches/complete and the match detail (expiring).
  Navigation: G.
  Multi-account: the countdown parity.
K. ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
Generic acceptance (all must hold):

PHASE 12 - Messaging A: Inbox and the Three-Panel Layout
Purpose:  A responsive inbox and three-panel chat layout (conversations / conversation / profile context) whose composer stays visible at every zoom.
Priority: P1

B. SCOPE
  - Routes: /chats (INBOX, CHAT-EMPTY), /chats/<nickname> (the layout only; conversation contents are Phase 13, the menu is Phase 14).
  - Components:
      src/components/sections/portal/chats/inbox-view.tsx (904 + 352)
      conversation-list.tsx (280 px list)
      conversation-context.tsx (312 px right panel, "Flow context / Conversation", image object-contain)

      pieces member-row (photo 48, meta time, unread badge), portal-card, workspace
  - APIs: GET /conversations?cursor&limit, GET /conversations/:id, socket room join for listed rooms (message:new, conversation closed), POST /conversations/:id/seen (unread counts).
C. INITIAL AUDIT
  - The three panels at 100 % (1440), 110 % (1309, still xl: the 280 + 312 fixed columns leave about 600 px for the conversation), 125 % (1152, lg: the context is hidden, with no way to open it), 150-175 % (single pane), 200-400 % (single pane, 225-450 px tall: the 420 px pane floor is taller than the viewport, so the composer is pushed off-screen?).
  - At 50 % (2880 px): the panes cap at 760 px tall and the columns are fixed, so check the empty space.
  - OWNER REPORT (2026-11-16): at 100 % in a normal browser the messaging section looks oversized (type, paddings and the header / action rows feel too big). The message box where the conversation shows is so short that only one or two messages are visible and everything else needs scrolling. Measure at 1440x900, 1309x818 and 1152x720: the height of the message list (the scroller) versus everything stacked above and below it inside the pane (conversation header, Figma action row, tools, composer), and the type sizes against the Figma W05 frame and the token scale. Write the numbers into the report before changing code.
  - Preserve: the list joining every room live, the unread counts, CHAT-EMPTY, the conversation lookup by nickname in the cached list (one request).
D. IMPLEMENTATION
  1. Three-panel layout:
       >=1280 CSS px: list | conversation | context, with clamp-based side columns (list clamp(240px, 20%, 320px), context clamp(260px, 22%, 360px)) and the conversation minmax(0,1fr).
       1024-1279: list | conversation. The context opens as a right drawer from an info button in the conversation header (PortalDialog side variant or a new Drawer piece built on the Radix Dialog already installed); it doesn't vanish.
       <1024: a single pane. /chats shows the list; /chats/<member> shows the conversation with the context in the drawer.
  2. Pane height: replace the fixed 760 px / 420 px floor with height = 100dvh minus the header, the tab bar and the page padding (as CSS variables from Phase 1), with a minimum that still fits a 225 px viewport. The message list is the only scroller, and the composer is always visible at the bottom of the pane.
  3. At 50 % zoom: the panes grow in height up to a sensible cap and the columns widen through clamp; no fixed 760 px.
  4. Conversation list rows: truncate names and previews with min-w-0; time and unread badge never overflow.
  5. Message area first (owner report). The message list gets the largest share of the pane: at 1440x900 (100 %) it shows at least 6-8 ordinary one-line messages without scrolling, and at 1309 / 1152 proportionally as many. Get there by compacting what surrounds it (header row, the Figma action row, the tools that Phase 13 moves into the "+" menu, composer paddings), and by bringing chat type and spacing that is larger than the Figma W05 frame back to the Figma size through the scale tokens. Never by shrinking text below Figma or below readable sizes. Keep px type (rules 4 / 24). The Figma frame is a reference, not a blind spec: keep the owner's later changes and make the result look polished. Record before / after list heights and visible message counts in the report.
E. RESPONSIVENESS
Zoom levels (all mandatory): 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 % at a 1440x900 window, then spot-check 50 %, 200 % and 400 % at a 1920x1080 window. Also check the rule 3 widths 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920. (Equivalent CSS viewports are in standing rule 3.)
At every zoom, record and fix:
  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;
  - focus ring and keyboard order broken at high zoom.
  Phase-specific (this is the key acceptance):
  - the composer and Send are visible without scrolling the page at all 13 zoom levels (with a minimal composer stub until Phase 13 finishes);
  - the panel transitions happen at 1280 and 1024 with no overflow;
  - the drawer is usable at 360x225.
