0. Context. Repo: c:\Users\fardin\Downloads\TECHREION\48date-user, the 48Date user web frontend. Stack: Next.js 16.4 App Router (cacheComponents / partial prerendering, React Compiler, Turbopack), React 19.3, Tailwind CSS v4, TanStack Query v5, socket.io-client, Vitest 3 (node environment). This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. When a fix needs the backend, record it under "Backend/API blockers" in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md: the endpoint, the current behaviour, the needed behaviour and the frontend workaround (if any).

1. Read first:
   - AGENTS.md: binding rules 1-45, the "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" rules 46-58, the Pieces registry and the Decisions list;
   - CLAUDE.md and README.md;
   - the bundled Next.js guide in node_modules/next/dist/docs/ for every Next API you touch (this Next version has breaking changes);
   - NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md, to see what earlier phases did. Do NOT edit the older FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md.

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

C. INITIAL AUDIT
  - Bubbles: own vs incoming alignment, width = 100 % (N5), long words or URLs overflowing, timestamp lines always visible, the photo attachment cropped to 4:5.
  - Tools: 4 pills + a separate CHAT-ATTACH chooser; Plan a date / Play a game appear in several places (action row secondary buttons, the attach chooser, MSG-01).
  - The composer at 360x225: textarea growth and the Send button.
  - Preserve: delivery and seen states ("Sent / Seen time / Not sent, Tap Retry"), Retry without duplicates (clientMessageId), Edit putting the text back, the QUOTA gate on the first hello for FREE members, the microphone and photo rules (rule 37), AI flows and AI-ERROR, the typing indicator, edited / removed lines, the screen-specific primary action row from Figma (for example "Get a conversation starter", "Plan a date"). Keep that row; only the TOOLS move into the menu (record the decision).
D. IMPLEMENTATION
  1. Bubbles (chat-bubble.tsx):
       - The list item aligns by variant: own -> self-end / justify-end (right), incoming -> self-start (left).
       - The bubble is inline-flex, with width fitting the content up to max-w-[min(80%,36rem)] (a token).
       - Capsule styling: a large radius token (20-24 px; a smaller radius on the corner toward the sender as a tail).
       - Text uses whitespace-pre-wrap with overflow-wrap:anywhere.
       - Consecutive messages from the same side group with smaller gaps.
  2. Timestamps: hidden visually by default and revealed on hover, on focus-within or when the bubble is tapped (a toggle button around the bubble content with aria-expanded, or a disclosure). The time is always in the accessible name or in sr-only text. The delivery status of the LAST own message ("Sent" / "Seen") stays visible. A failed message always shows its "Not sent" line with Retry and Edit. The status keeps updating live.
  3. Photo attachments use MediaFrame (Phase 1): their own ratio, contained, with a max-height token. Voice notes are an audio element at max-w-full.
  4. "+" action menu (ActionMenu piece):
       - The trigger is a 44 px round button with the add-01 icon and aria-label "Conversation actions", aria-haspopup="menu" and aria-expanded, placed immediately left of the message input inside the composer.
       - The menu (role="menu", items role="menuitem" with icon + label) holds, in this order: AI help, Safety, Voice, Photo, Plan date, Play game. Each item calls the EXISTING handler or link: askAi, openBlock (Safety; Phase 14 may point it at the safety section of the menu), openVoice, the photo picker / CHAT-PHOTO, Plan date -> VENUES for the member (MemberLink via forMember), Play game -> GAMES.
       - Behaviour: opens on click or tap, and on Enter / Space / ArrowDown with focus on the first item. Arrow up / down, Home and End move focus. Escape closes and returns focus to the trigger. A click or tap outside closes it (prefer the native Popover API popover="auto" for light-dismiss, with a pointerdown-outside fallback). Tab closes it.
       - Placement: above the composer, flipped or clamped so it never clips. When the viewport is too short (max-height 420 px) or narrow, it renders as a bottom sheet.
       - Remove the old 4-pill tools grid and fold the CHAT-ATTACH chooser into the menu (keep the CHAT-ATTACH state only if Figma flows need it; otherwise record the replacement in Decisions).
       - No new dependency.
  5. Composer: the textarea auto-grows up to about 5 lines (token) then scrolls. Send is always visible and disabled while empty or sending. Keep the existing Enter / Shift+Enter behaviour (verify it) and an aria-label for Send. The composer sits inside the Phase 12 pane so it never leaves the viewport.
  6. OWNER-REPORTED BUG (2026-11-16): duplicate bubble after sending. Sending a message shows "Sending…"; once it is sent, TWO bubbles show for about a second (the sent message with "Sent" AND a second copy still reading "Sending…"), then the extra one vanishes. Cause found while writing this phase: chat-view.tsx send() renders a separate `pending` bubble (`{pending && <ChatBubble … meta={copy.sending} />}`) and clears it only after `await chatService.send(...)` AND `await refresh()`. The service already merges the sent message and notifies watchers (and the socket's message:new can arrive first), so the real message renders while `pending` is still set. Fix: one bubble per message for its whole life. The optimistic message carries its clientMessageId and is replaced in place by the server copy (or the socket copy) with the same id, so it goes "Sending…" -> "Sent" -> "Seen" without a second bubble, and never two copies when the POST response and message:new both arrive. The same applies to Retry, photo and voice sends. Add a test (src/lib/portal/chat-optimistic.test.ts): send, then server response, then socket echo (and the reverse order) gives exactly one message with the right status at each step.
  7. Fix every other bug found (scroll to the newest message on send or arrival, keeping position when loading earlier messages, no duplicate messages on reconnect).
E. RESPONSIVENESS
Zoom levels (all mandatory): 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 % at a 1440x900 window, then spot-check 50 %, 200 % and 400 % at a 1920x1080 window. Also check the rule 3 widths 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920. (Equivalent CSS viewports are in standing rule 3.)
At every zoom, record and fix:
  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;
  - focus ring and keyboard order broken at high zoom.
  Phase-specific:
  - bubbles never wider than the pane;
  - long unbroken strings wrap;
  - the menu never clips at 360x225 (becomes a sheet);
  - the composer and + button fit at 360 px;
  - the AI suggestion cards and the voice recorder fit at 400 %.

  - Avatars 1:1.

  - Plan date from the menu -> VENUES -> Back = this conversation (the key example).
  - Play game -> GAMES -> Back = this conversation.

A bug caused by the backend is written into "Backend/API blockers" with the endpoint, the observed response, the expected response and the frontend workaround.

STANDING RULES (apply to every step of this phase)

0. Context. Repo: c:\Users\fardin\Downloads\TECHREION\48date-user, the 48Date user web frontend. Stack: Next.js 16.4 App Router (cacheComponents / partial prerendering, React Compiler, Turbopack), React 19.3, Tailwind CSS v4, TanStack Query v5, socket.io-client, Vitest 3 (node environment). This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. When a fix needs the backend, record it under "Backend/API blockers" in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md: the endpoint, the current behaviour, the needed behaviour and the frontend workaround (if any).

1. Read first:
   - AGENTS.md: binding rules 1-45, the "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" rules 46-58, the Pieces registry and the Decisions list;
   - CLAUDE.md and README.md;
   - the bundled Next.js guide in node_modules/next/dist/docs/ for every Next API you touch (this Next version has breaking changes);
   - NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md, to see what earlier phases did. Do NOT edit the older FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md.

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:

PHASE 14 - Messaging C: Conversation Menu (Unmatch, Block / Unblock, Report)
Purpose:  A three-dot conversation menu with Unmatch, Block / Unblock and Report a Problem, wired to the real APIs with confirmations.
Priority: P1

B. SCOPE
  - Routes: /chats/<nickname> (the BLOCK dialog, BLOCK-DONE, the new UNMATCH confirmation); hand-off to /report/<nickname> (REPORT-DETAIL, Phase 30 owns that page) and to /settings/blocked (Phase 31).
  - Components: chat-view.tsx (conversation header), the ActionMenu piece from Phase 13 (reused with a three-dot trigger), portal-dialog; data/portal/chats.ts (block dialog copy).

  - Service: chatService.block / unblock (existing; rule 39 keeps blocking in chatService).
  - APIs: POST /blocks {nickname, source: "CHAT"}, DELETE /blocks/:nickname, GET /blocks (block state), POST /reports (Phase 30 page; source CHAT, conversationId, alsoBlock), socket conversation-closed event.
  - UNMATCH: the collection has NO unmatch endpoint (BACKEND BLOCKER N12).
C. INITIAL AUDIT
  - The current safety entry points (the Safety pill opens the BLOCK dialog; REPORT-DONE "Block this member"); BLOCK-DONE behaviour; how a blocked member's conversation behaves (does it leave INBOX / show CHAT-EMPTY?).

D. IMPLEMENTATION
  1. Add a 44 px three-dot button in the conversation header (aria-label "Conversation options") opening the ActionMenu with: "Unmatch", "Block {name}" / "Unblock {name}" (by the current block state) and "Report a problem".
  2. Block: open the existing BLOCK confirmation dialog, then POST /blocks, then BLOCK-DONE; the conversation leaves the list live; navigate with replaceRoute so Back can't reopen the blocked chat. Unblock (when the member is blocked and the screen is still showing, for example BLOCK-DONE): confirm, DELETE /blocks/:nickname, then invalidate the blocks and conversations keys.
  3. Report a problem: navigate to REPORT-DETAIL for this member (/report/<nickname>), passing the conversation context so the report sends source CHAT + conversationId; Back returns to the conversation.
  4. Unmatch: there is no API, so render the item through notFunctional("Unmatch needs a backend endpoint") as an aria-disabled menu item carrying that reason (rule 12). Do NOT fake it and do NOT reuse block. Add the service contract method (chatService.unmatch) only when the endpoint exists. Record the blocker with the proposed contract: POST /matches/:matchId/unmatch (or DELETE /matches/:matchId), which ends the match, closes the conversation, emits a socket "match:ended" / conversation-closed event to both sides, keeps the history hidden, and returns 200 with { matchId, endedAt }.
  5. The Safety item of the + menu (Phase 13) can open this same safety menu section or the block dialog; keep one implementation (no duplicated markup).
E. RESPONSIVENESS
Zoom levels (all mandatory): 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 % at a 1440x900 window, then spot-check 50 %, 200 % and 400 % at a 1920x1080 window. Also check the rule 3 widths 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920. (Equivalent CSS viewports are in standing rule 3.)
At every zoom, record and fix:
  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;
  - focus ring and keyboard order broken at high zoom.
  Phase-specific: the three-dot menu and the confirmation dialogs at 360x225 (sheet / scrollable dialog with visible actions); the header with name + info + three-dot at 360 px.
F. IMAGES
  N/A for the menu itself (icons only). The BLOCK-DONE artwork, if it has a photo, is 1:1 cover; check it isn't stretched.
G. BACK NAVIGATION
  - After a block, Back goes to INBOX (replace, so the blocked conversation isn't reopened).
  - Report -> Back = the conversation; report submitted -> REPORT-DONE -> "Back to matches" / "Block this member" (existing).
  - A direct visit to a blocked member's chat shows the unavailable / closed state.
H. FUNCTIONAL AND API
  - Block / unblock round trips (GET /blocks reflects them), the report hand-off with the correct source, the menu state reflecting the block status, the socket closing the conversation on the other side.
  - Multi-account: M3 blocks F7 from the chat: F7's conversation closes live (no new messages possible) and the conversation leaves both inboxes. M3 then unblocks (here or in /settings/blocked); record whether the backend restores the match / conversation (backend behaviour, not a frontend bug). M2 reports F6 with "Report a problem" (use a mild reason, no alsoBlock), then verify the report was accepted (201) and nothing else changed.
I. BUG FIXING
Find and fix EVERY frontend bug inside this phase's scope, not only those listed: layout, state, logic, accessibility, API mapping, loading / empty / error states, stale cache after writes, race conditions, console errors and warnings, wrong copy wiring and broken links. Before changing a shared piece or helper, grep every caller and re-test them. A bug outside the scope is written into the QA report "Remaining issues", not fixed here. A bug caused by the backend is written into "Backend/API blockers" with the endpoint, the observed response, the expected response and the frontend workaround.
J. TEST CASES
Existing tests to run UNCHANGED (baseline 2026-11-16: 3 files, 54 tests, all passing): src/lib/api/api-layer.test.ts (12), src/lib/portal/portal-logic.test.ts (10), src/test/api-contract.test.ts (32), plus every *.test.ts added by earlier phases. Never edit, skip, weaken or delete them. Then run npm run lint and npm run build (baseline: both clean).
  New tests:
  - src/lib/portal/conversation-menu.test.ts: the item list by block state; Unmatch is notFunctional while no endpoint exists.
  - A contract test for POST /blocks and DELETE /blocks/:nickname (new file, if not covered).
  Zoom: the matrix on the open three-dot menu, the BLOCK dialog and BLOCK-DONE.
  Navigation: G.
  Multi-account: block / unblock and report.
K. ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
Generic acceptance (all must hold):
  [ ] Every route and state in scope passes the 13-level zoom matrix and the rule 3 widths: no horizontal overflow, no unintended bands, no clipping, the primary action reachable. The results table is in the QA report.
  [ ] Every Back in scope behaves as listed in G, including direct entry and refresh.
  [ ] Every image in scope keeps its intended ratio, with no stretching and no unintended cropping (or the phase says why images are N/A).

STANDING RULES (apply to every step of this phase)

0. Context. Repo: c:\Users\fardin\Downloads\TECHREION\48date-user, the 48Date user web frontend. Stack: Next.js 16.4 App Router (cacheComponents / partial prerendering, React Compiler, Turbopack), React 19.3, Tailwind CSS v4, TanStack Query v5, socket.io-client, Vitest 3 (node environment). This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. When a fix needs the backend, record it under "Backend/API blockers" in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md: the endpoint, the current behaviour, the needed behaviour and the frontend workaround (if any).

1. Read first:
   - AGENTS.md: binding rules 1-45, the "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" rules 46-58, the Pieces registry and the Decisions list;
   - CLAUDE.md and README.md;
   - the bundled Next.js guide in node_modules/next/dist/docs/ for every Next API you touch (this Next version has breaking changes);
   - NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md, to see what earlier phases did. Do NOT edit the older FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md.

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.
   - When a test fails: find the cause, fix the frontend, and re-run the unchanged test. If the cause is the backend, record it as a blocker.
   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.
   - Zoom and layout checks that Vitest can't run go into the QA report as a manual matrix with their results (or into Playwright specs, only if Playwright has been approved and installed).
   - Finish with npm test, npm run lint and npm run build. All must pass with no new warnings.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;
    - bugs found and bugs fixed;
    - tests executed, passed and failed (the real numbers from the run);
    - the zoom matrix results and the back-navigation results;
    - backend/API blockers and remaining issues.
    Also update the phase table. Never claim a test passed without running it.

Purpose:  Venue discovery for a date, with an independent date area (never the profile location), a responsive map and list, and correct Back.

  - APIs: POST /matches/:id/venue-suggestions {category}, GET /venue-suggestions/:runId, GET /venue-suggestions/:runId/events (SSE), GET /venue-suggestions/:runId/weather. NOT to be called here: PATCH /users/me/location.

D. IMPLEMENTATION
  1. Date-specific area (rule 55; requirement 7.3):
       a. Change both changeArea actions in data/portal/dates.ts from to("LOCATION") to a client state on the VENUES URL ("VENUES-AREA", rule 32), opened as a PortalDialog with area search (search field + result rows via OptionRow "area" + "Use my current location" from a click, rounded to 0.01 degrees, never stored) and a confirm action.
       b. dateService gets searchAreas(query) and setPlanArea(member, area). They reuse lib/onboarding/area-search.ts (no onboardingService call from portal screens) and the store's starter list.

       d. Venue discovery by area. The API takes NO location (BLOCKER N13: its midpoint is server-side). With the API, request suggestions as now, then rank / filter the returned venues by distance from the chosen area centre (haversine with the venues' lat / lng; show "{x} km from {area}"). When fewer than 1 venue is within a sensible radius, show VENUES-EMPTY with an honest note. Record the blocker, asking for POST /matches/:id/venue-suggestions to accept { latitude, longitude, radiusKm } or { areaLabel }. With the mock, filter the sample venues by area.

  2. Layout: map / list side by side >=1024 with clamp-based columns. Below that, the map is above the list with the map height as an aspect-ratio (no fixed px). Pins are positioned in % so they stay on the map at every zoom.
  3. The place card at 360 px: the photo column is fixed at 112 px only if the text column keeps >=160 px; otherwise the photo stacks on top.

   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.
   - When a test fails: find the cause, fix the frontend, and re-run the unchanged test. If the cause is the backend, record it as a blocker.
   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.
   - Zoom and layout checks that Vitest can't run go into the QA report as a manual matrix with their results (or into Playwright specs, only if Playwright has been approved and installed).
   - Finish with npm test, npm run lint and npm run build. All must pass with no new warnings.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;
    - bugs found and bugs fixed;
    - tests executed, passed and failed (the real numbers from the run);
    - the zoom matrix results and the back-navigation results;
    - backend/API blockers and remaining issues.
    Also update the phase table. Never claim a test passed without running it.

WORK ORDER
  1. Read the rules (1).
  2. Audit the scope. Write the findings into the report before changing code.
  3. Implement D-H.
  4. Write the new tests (J).
  5. Run the zoom matrix, the back-navigation checks and the functional and multi-account checks.
  6. Run npm test, npm run lint and npm run build, and fix frontend failures until they pass.
  7. Update the report.
  8. STOP. Summarise the changed files, the test numbers and the blockers, then wait for the next phase instruction.

-----8<----- BEGIN PHASE 16 PROMPT -----
You are acting as a Senior Frontend Engineer, UI/UX Engineer and QA Automation Engineer on the existing 48Date web frontend. Execute ONLY Phase 16 ("Date Time, Review and Sending the Invitation") of NEW_FRONTEND_IMPROVEMENT_ROADMAP.txt. Do not start any other phase.

PHASE 16 - Date Time, Review and Sending the Invitation
Purpose:  Pick a day and time, review and send a real date invitation, responsive at every zoom, with correct Back and validation.
Priority: P1

B. SCOPE
  - Routes: /dates/time/<nickname> (DATE-TIME with the DATE-PICKER and TIME-PICKER dialogs), /dates/review/<nickname> (DATE-REVIEW with the weather hint).
  - Components: time-view.tsx, review-view.tsx, date-summary.tsx, date-context.tsx; pieces date-picker (Monday first, past days disabled), picker-button, select-field (timezone), option-row, summary-row, detail-row, portal-dialog; lib/portal/date-format.ts.
  - APIs: POST /matches/:id/dates {scheduledAt in the plan's timezone, venueName, venueAddress, venueCategory, venueRef, latitude, longitude, runId}, GET /venue-suggestions/:runId/weather?day&venueRef.
C. INITIAL AUDIT
  - The picker dialogs at 360x225 (the calendar grid fits?); the time list scrolling; the summary rows at 360 px; the send button reachable.
  - Validation: the API requires 1 hour to 14 days ahead. Is that reflected in the picker (max day) and the error copy?
  - Preserve: suggested days are the next 3; 4:00 PM default; half-hours 10:00 AM-9:00 PM; timezone list; review shows the selected area (from Phase 15) and the venue.
D. IMPLEMENTATION
  1. The DatePicker max = today + 14 days, and times less than 1 hour from now are disabled, so client validation matches the server. The server's 4xx message still shows through failureReason.
  2. The review shows: venue, address, date, time, timezone, the date-specific area (Phase 15) and the weather hint.
  3. Sending: POST, then navigate to DATE-PENDING (/dates/plan/<member>) with replaceRoute so Back doesn't return to a sent review; invalidate the match and plan keys.
  4. Dialogs: scrollable bodies with visible actions; the calendar cells keep 44 px targets or the grid scrolls inside the dialog.
E. RESPONSIVENESS
Zoom levels (all mandatory): 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 % at a 1440x900 window, then spot-check 50 %, 200 % and 400 % at a 1920x1080 window. Also check the rule 3 widths 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920. (Equivalent CSS viewports are in standing rule 3.)
At every zoom, record and fix:
  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;
  - focus ring and keyboard order broken at high zoom.
  Phase-specific: the calendar dialog and time list at 360x225; the review summary layout (split -> column rows at narrow widths).
F. IMAGES
  - The date-context member photo (cover, centre-top) and the venue image in the review (the Figma frame ratio, cover). Test portrait and landscape.
G. BACK NAVIGATION
  - TIME -> Back = PLACE (or VENUES when entered from there); REVIEW -> Back = TIME.
  - The draft (venue, day, time, timezone, area) is kept on Back and on refresh.
  - After sending, Back from the plan goes to the conversation (the review was replaced).
  - Direct entry to /dates/review/<member> without a complete draft redirects to the missing step.
H. FUNCTIONAL AND API
  - Real invitation creation; only one open plan per match (an API error when one exists, shown honestly); the timezone conversion of scheduledAt; the weather hint.
  - Multi-account: M1 sends F5 an invitation for tomorrow 4:00 PM at a suggested venue. F5 receives the chat invitation card and the DATE_INVITE_RECEIVED notification (verify in Phase 24 too). The card shows the same day and time in F5's timezone.
I. BUG FIXING
Find and fix EVERY frontend bug inside this phase's scope, not only those listed: layout, state, logic, accessibility, API mapping, loading / empty / error states, stale cache after writes, race conditions, console errors and warnings, wrong copy wiring and broken links. Before changing a shared piece or helper, grep every caller and re-test them. A bug outside the scope is written into the QA report "Remaining issues", not fixed here. A bug caused by the backend is written into "Backend/API blockers" with the endpoint, the observed response, the expected response and the frontend workaround.
J. TEST CASES
Existing tests to run UNCHANGED (baseline 2026-11-16: 3 files, 54 tests, all passing): src/lib/api/api-layer.test.ts (12), src/lib/portal/portal-logic.test.ts (10), src/test/api-contract.test.ts (32), plus every *.test.ts added by earlier phases. Never edit, skip, weaken or delete them. Then run npm run lint and npm run build (baseline: both clean).
  New tests:
  - src/lib/portal/date-format.test.ts: scheduledAt from day + time + timezone (including a DST edge); the 1 h / 14 d window; the past-time disabling.
  - A contract test for POST /matches/:id/dates and the weather (new file, if not covered).
  Zoom: the matrix on DATE-TIME (both dialogs open) and DATE-REVIEW.
  Navigation: G.
  Multi-account: M1 -> F5 invitation.
K. ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
Generic acceptance (all must hold):
  [ ] Every route and state in scope passes the 13-level zoom matrix and the rule 3 widths: no horizontal overflow, no unintended bands, no clipping, the primary action reachable. The results table is in the QA report.
  [ ] Every Back in scope behaves as listed in G, including direct entry and refresh.
  [ ] Every image in scope keeps its intended ratio, with no stretching and no unintended cropping (or the phase says why images are N/A).
  [ ] Every API-driven view in scope shows real API data and has loading, empty and error states. Writes update the UI from the server response or by invalidating the cache.

This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.

     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.

PHASE 17 - Date Plan States, Accepting and Managing a Date
Purpose:  Every pre-date plan state (pending, received, confirmed, declined, cancelled, expired, postponed) live from the server, with accept / decline / cancel / postpone, responsive and with correct Back.
Priority: P1

B. SCOPE
  - Routes: /dates/plan/<nickname>: DATE-PENDING, the received invitation ("You're invited"), DATE-CONFIRMED (before the date time), DATE-DECLINED, DATE-CANCELLED, DATE-INVITE-EXPIRED, the postpone request (Accept / Keep original / Withdraw). /dates/manage/<nickname>: DATE-MANAGE with the DATE-CANCEL and SELECT-CANCEL dialogs.

Unused: GET /dates/:id/history (an optional "Plan history" list on DATE-MANAGE, design-silent; leave it to Phase 36 unless the owner wants it here).

D. IMPLEMENTATION
  1. DATE-CONFIRMED hierarchy: the first block is a "date details" card with the venue name (h2), date, time, timezone, the selected area, address / meeting place and a map link, if the data exists. Status badges and secondary actions (manage, conversation) come after.
  2. Every state's StatusScreen / PlanDetails: the actions reachable at 400 %, summary rows wrapping, no fixed heights.
  3. Manage: the cancel reasons dialog (SELECT-CANCEL) scrolls at short heights. Cancel success replaces to the plan / cancelled state.
  4. Live: confirm dateService.watch updates the open plan when the partner accepts, declines or postpones, without a reload.
E. RESPONSIVENESS
Zoom levels (all mandatory): 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 % at a 1440x900 window, then spot-check 50 %, 200 % and 400 % at a 1920x1080 window. Also check the rule 3 widths 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920. (Equivalent CSS viewports are in standing rule 3.)
At every zoom, record and fix:
  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;

  - Notifications -> plan -> Back = Notifications.
  - Match detail -> plan -> Back = Match detail.
  - Manage -> Back = plan.
  - After cancel, decline or withdraw: replace (Back doesn't reopen the manage dialog).

H. FUNCTIONAL AND API

      F5 accepts M1's invitation, and M1's open plan switches to confirmed live.
      M1 requests a postpone and F5 sees the request with Accept / Keep original.
      F5 keeps the original.
      Finally cancel only if the test data must be reset (record it).

A bug caused by the backend is written into "Backend/API blockers" with the endpoint, the observed response, the expected response and the frontend workaround.

STANDING RULES (apply to every step of this phase)

0. Context. Repo: c:\Users\fardin\Downloads\TECHREION\48date-user, the 48Date user web frontend. Stack: Next.js 16.4 App Router (cacheComponents / partial prerendering, React Compiler, Turbopack), React 19.3, Tailwind CSS v4, TanStack Query v5, socket.io-client, Vitest 3 (node environment). This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. When a fix needs the backend, record it under "Backend/API blockers" in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md: the endpoint, the current behaviour, the needed behaviour and the frontend workaround (if any).

1. Read first:
   - AGENTS.md: binding rules 1-45, the "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" rules 46-58, the Pieces registry and the Decisions list;
   - CLAUDE.md and README.md;
   - the bundled Next.js guide in node_modules/next/dist/docs/ for every Next API you touch (this Next version has breaking changes);
   - NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md, to see what earlier phases did. Do NOT edit the older FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md.

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.
   - When a test fails: find the cause, fix the frontend, and re-run the unchanged test. If the cause is the backend, record it as a blocker.
   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.
   - Zoom and layout checks that Vitest can't run go into the QA report as a manual matrix with their results (or into Playwright specs, only if Playwright has been approved and installed).
   - Finish with npm test, npm run lint and npm run build. All must pass with no new warnings.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;
    - bugs found and bugs fixed;
    - tests executed, passed and failed (the real numbers from the run);
    - the zoom matrix results and the back-navigation results;
    - backend/API blockers and remaining issues.

  Zoom: the matrix on ATTENDANCE, NO-SHOW, ATTENDANCE-WAIT, FEEDBACK and DATE-COMPLETE.
  Navigation: G.
K. ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
Generic acceptance (all must hold):
  [ ] Every route and state in scope passes the 13-level zoom matrix and the rule 3 widths: no horizontal overflow, no unintended bands, no clipping, the primary action reachable. The results table is in the QA report.
