  [ ] Every route and state in scope passes the 13-level zoom matrix and the rule 3 widths: no horizontal overflow, no unintended bands, no clipping, the primary action reachable. The results table is in the QA report.
  [ ] Every Back in scope behaves as listed in G, including direct entry and refresh.
  [ ] Every image in scope keeps its intended ratio, with no stretching and no unintended cropping (or the phase says why images are N/A).
  [ ] Every API-driven view in scope shows real API data and has loading, empty and error states. Writes update the UI from the server response or by invalidating the cache.
  [ ] The new tests listed in J exist and pass. All existing tests pass unchanged.
  [ ] npm test, npm run lint and npm run build pass with no new warnings.
  [ ] The AGENTS.md Pieces registry and Decisions are updated for every new piece or design-silent choice.
  [ ] NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md is updated (phase section and phase table). Backend/API blockers are listed separately.
  [ ] No backend code, no unrelated pages changed, no new dependency without approval, nothing committed.
  Phase-specific:
  [ ] Only the owner-approved items are built.
  [ ] Each is fully layered and tested.
  [ ] The rest are recorded.

STANDING RULES (apply to every step of this phase)

0. Context. Repo: c:\Users\fardin\Downloads\TECHREION\48date-user, the 48Date user web frontend. Stack: Next.js 16.4 App Router (cacheComponents / partial prerendering, React Compiler, Turbopack), React 19.3, Tailwind CSS v4, TanStack Query v5, socket.io-client, Vitest 3 (node environment). This repo is FRONTEND ONLY: never create or edit backend code. When a fix needs the backend, record it under "Backend/API blockers" in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md: the endpoint, the current behaviour, the needed behaviour and the frontend workaround (if any).

1. Read first:
   - AGENTS.md: binding rules 1-45, the "Frontend Responsiveness, Image, Navigation and QA Standards" rules 46-58, the Pieces registry and the Decisions list;
   - CLAUDE.md and README.md;
   - the bundled Next.js guide in node_modules/next/dist/docs/ for every Next API you touch (this Next version has breaking changes);
   - NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md, to see what earlier phases did. Do NOT edit the older FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md.

2. Keep the architecture:
   - Services. Components call only their feature service (authService, onboardingService, portalService, chatService, dateService, gameService, storyService, accountService, membershipService). Each service picks "apiEnabled ? api...Service : mock...Service". API services call src/lib/api/modules/*, which call "api" from src/lib/api/client.ts with paths from src/lib/api/endpoints.ts. Components never call fetch, lib/api or a path.
   - New endpoint. Add:
     a. the path to endpoints.ts;
     b. the types to lib/api/types/<module>.ts, field for field with postman/48Date-Backend.postman_collection.json (case-sensitive enums);
     c. a module function;
     d. the service contract method, its api implementation and a clearly marked mock implementation;
     e. a key in lib/screen-keys.ts;
     f. a contract test in a NEW test file.
   - Server data. Read it with useLoaded(screenKeys.x, service.fn) on TanStack Query, never useEffect + useState. Writes call invalidate(...) or queryClient.setQueryData. Live data comes from SSE (api.stream) or src/lib/api/socket.ts. Never add polling.
   - Content. Copy, image paths, alt text, icon slugs and actions live in src/data/** and are read through src/stores/**. Links use hrefFor / to / toItem from src/lib/routes.ts. A destination that doesn't exist yet uses notFunctional(reason).
   - Pieces live in src/components/pieces/<kind>/: one per file, kebab-case, named exports, with revealDelay and a placement-only className. Visual differences are props (size / variant / tone).
   - Tokens live in src/app/globals.css AND in lib/type-scale.ts, lib/control-sizes.ts or lib/tones.ts. No raw hex, px literals or copy in components; arbitrary values only for one-off Figma geometry. Merge classes with cn().
   - Icons are Hugeicons slugs rendered by Icon from public/icons/<slug>.svg. Export a missing glyph once from Figma (file L4MtWjAVlPSRAjJSzI1lrf, Solid Rounded), normalised to a full-box viewBox.
   - Routing is path-based, never #hash. Interaction states are client states on their step's URL. Flow data (phone number, drafts, plan) never goes in the URL.
   - Record every new or changed piece in the AGENTS.md Pieces registry, and every design-silent choice under "Decisions".

3. Browser-zoom protocol (rule 46).
   - Test levels: 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 %.
   - Preferred: real browser zoom (Ctrl + / Ctrl -) in a real Chrome window at 1440x900, then 1920x1080, with whatever browser tool this session has (Claude in Chrome, Chrome DevTools MCP, the built-in browser, or Playwright if it has been approved and installed).
   - Equivalent CSS viewport at a 1440x900 window. Use it to reason about breakpoints, and as the fallback when only viewport emulation exists (set deviceScaleFactor to the zoom and say so in the report):
       50 % = 2880x1800, 75 % = 1920x1200, 90 % = 1600x1000, 100 % = 1440x900, 110 % = 1309x818, 125 % = 1152x720, 150 % = 960x600, 175 % = 823x514, 200 % = 720x450, 250 % = 576x360, 300 % = 480x300, 350 % = 411x257, 400 % = 360x225.
     At a 1920x1080 window: 50 % = 3840x2160, 200 % = 960x540, 400 % = 480x270.
   - Breakpoints this maps onto: sm 640, md 768, lg 1024, xl 1280. So 110-125 % at 1440 is the 1024-1279 tablet layout, 150-175 % is 768-1023, and 250 % and above is the phone layout with the bottom tab bar.
   - Height is the hidden trap. At 300-400 % the viewport is only 225-300 CSS px tall. The header, the fixed phone tab bar, sticky composers and dialogs must not cover the content, every dialog body must scroll, and the primary action must stay reachable.
   - Fill the page at every zoom, top to bottom (rules 46-49, added 2026-11-16). No unintended empty space anywhere on a page: not beside the content and not below it.
       a. Zoom OUT (50-90 %): past its design frame (portal 1440, landing 1920) the page scales as ONE piece through the body zoom-fit in globals.css (add the page's fit class, e.g. "fit-portal"; use var(--svh), never raw svh / vh, for heights inside it). Every column, card, text and gap grows in proportion; never let one component grow alone while the rest stay fixed, and never leave a grey band at the sides or the bottom.
       b. Short pages: the shell is a full-height flex column and the page's primary surface (Workspace panel, chat panes) stretches to the bottom of the viewport.
       c. Zoom IN (200-400 %): the page's main thing leads and stays in view - the composer and Send in a conversation, the profile photo, details and Like / Pass on Discover and Likes, the plan's primary action in date planning, Choose plan in membership. Use usePrimaryInView (src/hooks) to bring the primary region into view on open in stacked layouts, layout.stickyActions (lib/control-sizes.ts) to pin its controls above the tab bar, and order the content so the primary region comes before secondary panels on narrow screens.
       d. Record the bottom-gap and primary-in-view results for every route in the zoom matrix.
   - Fix techniques, in order of preference:
       a. Grid / Flex with minmax(0,1fr) and min-w-0;
       b. clamp() / min() / max() / fr for columns that must shrink;
       c. max-w-full on media;
       d. @container queries for pieces reused in columns of different widths;
       e. overflow-wrap:anywhere for user text;
       f. at narrow widths, stack columns, collapse sidebars, and move secondary panels below the content or into a drawer or disclosure.
   - Type stays px (rules 4 / 24). Browser zoom scales px; use rem only where a user font-size preference must be honoured, and record why. Never "fix" by deleting design spacing or by forcing everything to width:100%.

4. Back-navigation protocol (rules 51-53).
   - Use the shared back mechanism from Phase 1: src/lib/navigation/ (the per-tab trail), the useBackTarget hook and PortalBack, which go history-first and fall back to the data Action.to.
   - Phase 1 builds this mechanism (and MediaFrame). In any later phase, if it does not exist yet, STOP and report "Phase 1 must run first".
   - Back returns to the in-app page the member came from. The data "to" is only the fallback, for direct entry, refresh, external entry or a notification.
   - After an irreversible step (a sent invitation, a payment, deletion, a block, sign-out), navigate with replace so Back can't reopen the finished step.
   - Preserve the tab or URL segment, filters, scroll position, deck position, the open conversation and the date-plan draft.
   - Onboarding steps keep their Back targets from data/onboarding/flow.ts (rule 35).

5. Image protocol (rule 50).
   - Every image keeps the ratio of its purpose:
       - member portraits: 4:5 or 3:4, object-cover, focused on the face (object-top or centre-top);
       - avatars: 1:1, cover;
       - venue photos: their Figma frame, cover;
       - map art and illustrations: object-contain;
       - user-uploaded story and chat photos: their OWN ratio, measured from naturalWidth / naturalHeight on load (or taken from API width / height when present) and shown uncropped with the shared MediaFrame piece inside a max-height frame.
   - Never stretch an image, and never put both a fixed width and a fixed height around a photo whose ratio is unknown.
   - Use next/image with an accurate sizes. API photos are unoptimized via isRawImage; resolve keys with mediaUrl().

6. API protocol (rules 41-45, 54). For every endpoint in scope, verify:
   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

7. Test accounts, for multi-account checks.
   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.
   - Male: M1 +8801811000401 Omar Farooq @omar_farooq (Software Engineer, Gulshan, VERIFIED, PREMIUM); M2 +8801811000402 Rohan Sen @rohan_sen (Architect, Banani, VERIFIED, FREE); M3 +8801811000403 Julian Vance @julian_vance (Photographer, Dhanmondi, VERIFIED, PREMIUM, interestedIn EVERYONE).
   - Sign in through the existing setup: the API's test OTP hint, shown while the API sends devOtp. Do not guess passwords or OTPs.
   - Use one isolated browser context per account. Never sign the same account in twice: the backend keeps one session per account and revokes every session on refresh-token reuse.
   - Change account data only as far as the test needs, and restore preferences you changed.
   - No real payments, no external communication, and no OTPs, tokens or session data in screenshots or reports.
   - Run the frontend with "npm run dev -- -p 3001"; the backend owns port 3000. Settings live in .env.local.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.
   - When a test fails: find the cause, fix the frontend, and re-run the unchanged test. If the cause is the backend, record it as a blocker.
   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.
   - Zoom and layout checks that Vitest can't run go into the QA report as a manual matrix with their results (or into Playwright specs, only if Playwright has been approved and installed).
   - Finish with npm test, npm run lint and npm run build. All must pass with no new warnings.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;
    - bugs found and bugs fixed;
    - tests executed, passed and failed (the real numbers from the run);
    - the zoom matrix results and the back-navigation results;
    - backend/API blockers and remaining issues.
    Also update the phase table. Never claim a test passed without running it.

WORK ORDER
  1. Read the rules (1).
  2. Audit the scope. Write the findings into the report before changing code.
  3. Implement D-H.
  4. Write the new tests (J).
  5. Run the zoom matrix, the back-navigation checks and the functional and multi-account checks.
  6. Run npm test, npm run lint and npm run build, and fix frontend failures until they pass.
  7. Update the report.
  8. STOP. Summarise the changed files, the test numbers and the blockers, then wait for the next phase instruction.

  4. The multi-account journey (isolated contexts):
       a. M2 and F6: discover -> F6 likes M2 -> M2 /likes -> Like -> match (both);
       b. M2 <-> F6 chat: alignment, seen, typing, timestamps on hover / tap, the + menu (AI help, photo 9:16, voice, plan date, play game);
       c. M1 -> F5: date planning with a date-specific area (the profile location is unchanged) -> invitation -> F5 notification -> accept -> confirmed details first;

       e. story + partner consent (if the data allows);
       f. M3 blocks F7 from the three-dot menu -> unblock in settings;

       h. membership display for M1 / M2 / M3;
       i. sign-out in a context.
     Restore every changed preference and profile value.

E. RESPONSIVENESS
Zoom levels (all mandatory): 50, 75, 90, 100, 110, 125, 150, 175, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 % at a 1440x900 window, then spot-check 50 %, 200 % and 400 % at a 1920x1080 window. Also check the rule 3 widths 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920. (Equivalent CSS viewports are in standing rule 3.)
At every zoom, record and fix:
  - horizontal overflow (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth must be false);
  - unintended empty left/right bands, or containers that stop growing or shrinking;
  - overlapping, clipped or truncated text, buttons and badges;
  - the primary action not reachable (covered by the header, the fixed tab bar or a sticky bar, or pushed off-screen in a 225-300 px tall viewport);
  - sidebars and secondary panels that don't collapse, stack or move into a drawer at 200 % and above;
  - fixed px widths or heights that don't fit 360 CSS px;
  - excessive gaps or margins at 50-75 % that leave the page looking empty;
  - focus ring and keyboard order broken at high zoom.
  Phase-specific: the complete matrix for all routes.

H. FUNCTIONAL AND API

I. BUG FIXING
Find and fix EVERY frontend bug inside this phase's scope, not only those listed: layout, state, logic, accessibility, API mapping, loading / empty / error states, stale cache after writes, race conditions, console errors and warnings, wrong copy wiring and broken links. Before changing a shared piece or helper, grep every caller and re-test them. A bug outside the scope is written into the QA report "Remaining issues", not fixed here. A bug caused by the backend is written into "Backend/API blockers" with the endpoint, the observed response, the expected response and the frontend workaround.
J. TEST CASES

   - the request shape and the response mapping;
   - the loading, empty and error states (failureReason shows the API's 4xx message);
   - 401 / 403 / 429 handling;
   - cache invalidation after writes;
   - that the screen shows exactly the API data (compare it with the network response).
   Never fake success. With the API on, no sample or mock copy shows (liveData). The Postman collections in postman/ are the contract; the Admin folder is never called.

   - Female: F5 +8801811000105 Zara Ahmed @zara_ahmed; F6 +8801811000106 Olivia Davis @olivia_davis; F7 +8801811000107 Maya Lin @maya_lin.

8. Scope. Do not modify pages outside this phase. If a shared piece must change, re-test every caller. Do not add dependencies without asking the user first. Do not commit or push.

9. Tests (rules 26, 56, 57).
   - NEVER edit, skip, weaken or delete existing tests.
   - When a test fails: find the cause, fix the frontend, and re-run the unchanged test. If the cause is the backend, record it as a blocker.
   - Add new Vitest files beside the code (src/**/*.test.ts, node environment) for every bug you fix and every new behaviour. Contract tests for endpoints replay the Postman examples through src/test/postman-replay.ts.
   - Zoom and layout checks that Vitest can't run go into the QA report as a manual matrix with their results (or into Playwright specs, only if Playwright has been approved and installed).
   - Finish with npm test, npm run lint and npm run build. All must pass with no new warnings.

10. Report (rule 58). Add or replace this phase's section in NEW_FRONTEND_QA_REPORT.md with:
    - status, routes completed and files changed;
    - bugs found and bugs fixed;
    - tests executed, passed and failed (the real numbers from the run);
    - the zoom matrix results and the back-navigation results;
    - backend/API blockers and remaining issues.
    Also update the phase table. Never claim a test passed without running it.

WORK ORDER
  1. Read the rules (1).
  2. Audit the scope. Write the findings into the report before changing code.
  3. Implement D-H.
  4. Write the new tests (J).
  5. Run the zoom matrix, the back-navigation checks and the functional and multi-account checks.
  6. Run npm test, npm run lint and npm run build, and fix frontend failures until they pass.
  7. Update the report.
  8. STOP. Summarise the changed files, the test numbers and the blockers, then wait for the next phase instruction.
